Flagged for Review

Unresolved matches, blocked sources and inconsistent official fields are published here. They do not block publication; they do block unsupported claims.

Needs reviewPages is still in legacy mode: a merge can replace a fresh deployment with the committed fallback filesObserved 2026-10-15: production run 38816949473 deployed a fresh 22-fixture snapshot at 22:25:28Z, then the legacy pages-build-deployment for the next merge published the committed files at 22:26:45Z, so the live complete-picks page showed the older 17-fixture snapshot until a later run. GET /pages reports build_type legacy. A PUT to switch it to workflow returned HTTP 403: that endpoint requires pages=write and administration=write and the token the sessions use has no Administration permission. Owner action, one click: Settings → Pages → Build and deployment → Source → GitHub Actions (admins and maintainers can change it). Until then the last production run after the last merge decides what is live, and it must be green.
api.github.comgithub.comdocs.github.com
NoticeHotspawn's lobby HTML is CDN-cached; the fixture API is liveRead from a GitHub-hosted runner on 2026-10-15: /lobby was 87-88 minutes old and /cs2-pickems 71-72 minutes old (X-Cache HIT, Age 4,257-5,283 s, identical bytes across three reads) while the fixture API sent no cache headers. A cache-busting query string returned a fresh render (X-Cache MISS, Age 0). Any fixture change inside the cache window therefore looked like 'lobby, CS2 lobby and fixture API disagree' and froze the inventory. The collector now requests the fresh render, retries once and, if it still disagrees, names the differing fixture IDs and the cache state in its error.

Needs review1 affected source recordsOwn network model: an explicitly uncalibrated last-resort line, priced only when the results graph clears every gateTo price fixtures where both sides lack an official rating and no book publishes a price, this session added a Bradley–Terry fit over bo3.gg finished series, expressed on Valve's 400-point logistic scale through teams that do hold official points. It is a research model, never odds. It fails closed on: fewer than 300 finished series in the graph, fewer than 10 anchored teams, fewer than 6 finished series for either side in the 120-day window, or a rating-difference one-sigma interval wider than 300 points. In the 2026-10-21T13:32Z run the graph held 4,605 usable series and 269 anchored teams. Zetta vs LOS NINOS still publishes nothing, and the diagnostic recorded why: Zetta (bo3.gg id 23468) is anchored on a Valve row and has 5 finished series in the window (needs 6), while LOS NINOS (id 25500) has no finished series in the window at all, so the fit holds no rating for it. Lowering the six-series threshold would therefore not price this fixture, so the threshold was left unchanged; the missing side, not the gate, is the blocker. The model publishes its ratings, sample sizes, standard error, interval and evidence hash on every row it does price, and the publisher replays the arithmetic before deploying.

Needs review2 affected source recordsWhen bo3.gg does not list a fixture, team identity comes from an exact unique name lookup, not from a start-time bindingThe original bo3.gg bridge requires one upcoming match with the same start time and a matching team name. Low-tier fixtures are often not listed, so the own network model accepts a weaker bridge: each side must match exactly one bo3.gg team by normalised name, otherwise the fixture stays unbindable. Rows that use it publish the bridge in their evidence. On 2026-10-21 the bridge bound Zetta (23468), LOS NINOS (25500), UPGRADE (25212) and BlinkDagger (246) by exact name, but bound neither side of the two CIS LAN fixtures: bo3.gg does hold exactly one team named CYBERSHOKE (id 7429) yet Hotspawn publishes the roster as 'ex-CYBERSHOKE', and an 'ex-' marker is not part of the bo3.gg name, so an exact match cannot be claimed from the name alone; 'flacons' returns count 0 in both spellings. Identity is not the only gap: bo3.gg publishes no fixture between 7429 and 246 (BlinkDagger's single recorded match is a cancelled 2026-02-12 game), and no 2026-10-21 fixture for UPGRADE, so those two sides have neither a results edge nor a bookmaker mirror on the reachable sources.

NoticeMarket discovery identities are not verified match probabilitiesThe visible Polymarket directory is windowed. The alias-aware researcher now also replays fresh retained CS2-tag identities from the featured collector's original paginated Gamma pages and successful request metadata. These are discovery candidates only: the selected original full-series event and both positive-size executable books must still be fetched and validated. An absent, stale, ambiguous, suspended, one-sided or too-wide line supplies no probability. Additional esportsbook endpoints can remain inaccessible or lack a provable fixture identity; Hotspawn popularity and HLTV fan votes are never substitutes. Every unresolved fixture remains in the complete inventory and its unresolved section.

NoticeDuplicate provider candidates fail closed rather than selecting the firstIndependent synthetic regressions demonstrated that earlier fallback resolution could select the first matching event from a provider, and that an earlier Thunderpick check compared outcome names to themselves instead of checking the enclosing event's team pair. Those demonstrated code paths are now guarded: multiple matching full-series events/markets or conflicting observations from one provider are rejected, while an exactly repeated identical observation counts only once. Another independently unique source can still resolve the match. This audit does not assert that a particular historical match price was wrong; no such source-level mismatch was independently proved.

NoticeUnattended access limitations on HLTV and certain esportsbooksAutomated requests to HLTV match/ranking pages and several esportsbook websites return Cloudflare challenges (HTTP 403) or geographic restrictions. These are automated scraping limitations rather than proof that fixtures or betting lines do not exist. To maintain integrity without hallucination, our system gracefully falls back through the documented 5-rung ladder (first-party books -> bo3.gg relay -> Valve official regional standings -> own results network model -> HLTV world ranking logistic model) and explicitly documents any unresolvable fixtures in the separate Unresolved register.

NoticeFinal-day start times are derived from HLTV page times cross-checked against Liquipedia countdownsHLTV rendered times in Eastern Daylight Time during this research window, so the page times (07:30 for the third-place decider, 11:00 for the grand final) are not UTC. Both were cross-checked against Liquipedia's EDT countdowns and against the countdowns printed on the HLTV match pages: 07:30 EDT = 11:30 UTC = 13:30 Malta (CEST), and 11:00 EDT = 15:00 UTC = 17:00 Malta. Hotspawn's own lock times, where the fixture appears in the Hotspawn inventory, remain the authoritative pick deadline and are shown on the match rows.

It never deploys, never writes to the published dashboard, restores every tracked data file before committing so only its report is added, and runs the repository's own collectors read-only to record the live state. It exists because the session sandbox has no egress to Hotspawn, HLTV or any bookmaker while the GitHub runner does. It can be deleted once the audit is done without affecting the four-hour publisher.

NoticeFixed: Pinnacle specials (Yes/No, Odd/Even, score grids) could be read as two-team series linesThe session probe found that scripts/pinnacle_lines.py accepted any two-participant matchup, so 'Yes'/'No' and 'Odd'/'Even' specials on the shared board could produce a quote whenever a caller did not itself pass team names. Session be84edde's TWC reads were unaffected (they always passed team names), but the new generic Pinnacle rung needed the guard. Participant text is now checked against the specials pattern before any price is read, with regression tests in tests/test_unresolved_rungs.py. Also hardened: price arrays keyed by participantId are realigned onto participant order (a latent mismatch when prices arrive keyed but the matchup is name-only).

Needs reviewThe public site served a 15 October snapshot after a push-triggered legacy Pages build overwrote the current artifactObserved 2026-10-21T20:02Z-20:04Z. GitHub Pages for this repository is configured with build_type=legacy, source main:/, and the configuration API answers HTTP 403 ('Resource not accessible by integration') to this workflow's token, so the mode cannot be changed from CI. A push to main starts the platform's 'pages build and deployment' job, which publishes the checked-in branch root. That root still contains generated HTML from 2026-10-15 plus data/latest.json generated_at 2026-10-15T08:36:13Z; the deployed artifact for the same commit was still queued. Deployment evidence: legacy deployment 7235981775 succeeded at 20:01:34Z for a506382c, while the workflow deployment 7235963529 for the same SHA completed at 20:00:43Z. The served /data/latest.json reported generated_at 2026-10-15T08:36:13Z with 80 records / 17 upcoming, i.e. six-day-old matches and picks. Mitigation added in .github/workflows/pages.yml: after the normal deploy and verification, the job waits 75 seconds, re-runs scripts.verify_live against the public URL, and republishes the verified artifact if the legacy build replaced it. This does not change the repository setting; it keeps the current snapshot published. No probability, match record or pick was altered.

NoticeThe fast republish job could have restored a day-old snapshot over a fresher live one; it is now gated on the live valuerepublish-last-verified runs first in every publisher run and republishes the newest SUCCESSFUL run's artifact, so a reader never lands on the checked-in branch root while the fresh build is running. Needs reviewhttps://buffedlizard55-lab.github.io/MaltaProj3/data/latest.json still serves the checked-in 2026-10-15 snapshot while the rendered pages are currentRead at 2026-10-23T07:05Z: that URL returns schema_version 1, generated_at 2026-10-15T08:36:13Z, inventory 80, upcoming 17 - the repository's checked-in replay fixture. This is by design, not a second outage: the root publisher deliberately never writes data/* into the git-tracked branch root (that is what keeps deterministic CI replay working), so GitHub's legacy Pages builder serves data/ from the branch, while the rendered HTML and the artifact-served data come from the deployed build. The consequence is real for a reader who follows the footer's 'Open data' link: the page under it is current and the JSON beside it is not. Nothing here is inferred from that file - every figure on this site comes from the deployed artifact. Recorded for review rather than silently narrowed, because the two paths disagree and a reader cannot tell which one they are looking at.

Dated registers from earlier sessions · retained for audit, not current findings

Everything below is an earlier session’s dated record. It is kept unchanged for provenance; the findings at the top of this page are the current ones.

Day-one review: 9z vs Aurora

Primary organizer result: — unverified. Secondary-source corroboration: HLTV reports 9z beat Aurora 2–0 at Thunderpick World Championship 2026, also listed on its match page. This is not relabeled as primary organizer verification. A historical prematch price was not captured here, so upset magnitude, closing-line value and retrospective pick accuracy remain — unverified.

What the report supports: HLTV describes 9z taking control on Inferno and Aurora struggling on Nuke. It also reports that Aurora had beaten 9z at EPL 24. That supports treating match-specific conditions as important, not assuming a prior head-to-head winner must repeat. This single result does not calibrate our model or establish a profitable contrarian strategy.

Aurora roster check — official dated snapshots

Valve’s 6 July snapshot lists MAJ3R, soulfly, Wicadia, woxic and XANTARES; its 3 August, 7 September and 5 October snapshots instead list Jimpphat, kyxsan, Wicadia, woxic and XANTARES. This verifies a difference in published roster listings, not the precise transfer/announcement date. The last three snapshots list the same five names. They do not confirm the match-day lineup, a change after 5 October, player onboarding, or the absence of a later change. Recent change date/player, rest and travel advantage: — unverified. The independently checked HLTV rebuild report attributes the additions of Jimpphat, kyxsan and coach ash to Aurora announcements. That secondary report corroborates the summer rebuild, not a new October change or its causal effect on this loss; no numerical probability adjustment is applied.

Fixed534-01 · publisher / regression gateThe four-hour publisher was blocked and the public site was frozen. The scheduled runs 39038670350, 39045650525, 39054246287 and 39058407711 all failed at the step 'Regression tests (repository fixtures, before any cache restore)' while the last successful scheduled run, 39031549764, completed at about 12:20Z. The cause was a single wall-clock-dependent assertion: scripts/build.py::shell decided staleness with datetime.now(timezone.utc) against a fixture whose inventory_observed_at was hard-coded to 2026-10-17T04:27:55Z, so the eight-hour freshness window closed at 2026-10-17T12:27:55Z. Because the regression tests run before the cache restore and before collection, that one expired assertion stopped every refresh.shell, matches_table, glance, summary, picks, coverage, scripts.build_origination.pick_row and scripts.build.build now take an explicit reference time, and one build passes a single reference time to every page. The wall-clock-dependent assertion pins its own reference time instead. New tests/test_freshness_determinism.py checks the injectability contract and re-runs the repository's time-sensitive test modules with the production clock pinned to four absolute instants that bracket the fixture which broke the publisher, so the guard keeps working regardless of when it runs; it fails on the old wall-clock form at three of the four instants.
3 source links
  • Scheduled run that failed at the regression-test step; the four failing runs share this step and conclusion. github.com/buffedlizard55-lab/MaltaProj3
  • The last successful scheduled run, about 12:20Z, immediately before the eight-hour window closed at 12:27:55Z. github.com/buffedlizard55-lab/MaltaProj3
  • The publishing pipeline whose staleness decision now takes an injected reference time. github.com/buffedlizard55-lab/MaltaProj3

Platform limitationVD-04 · publishing / freshnessThe public site keeps reverting to the checked-in 15 October snapshot, because Pages is configured build_type=legacy with source main:/ and every push to main republishes the repository root. The checked-in root carries data/latest.json generated_at 2026-10-15T08:36:13Z (80 records, 17 upcoming), so for the ten-plus minutes the production workflow needs to collect, replay and build, the public site shows six-day-old matches and none of today's picks — and every new merge restarts that window, so while several sessions are merging the stale copy is what a reader usually gets. Observed repeatedly on 2026-10-22: at 01:30:26Z (eleven minutes after PR #146 merged), again after PR #150, and again at 04:25Z after the runs for d55ed87 and cd9a595 had been queued for more than half an hour, even though the workflow itself had deployed successfully (run 39717765225, both jobs success, 03:36Z). Two deployments of the same commit do conflict — the fixes that resolved that are described under handling — but fixing the deployment no longer stops the legacy build from overwriting the result.Three separate things were established and are recorded here rather than smoothed over. (1) The fast-republish job added in PR #150 deployed the same previous artifact first and made the fresh deployment fail at 'Run actions/deploy-pages@v5' in runs 39710589149, 39713153422, 39714834456 and 39715837365; deleting the duplicate artifact (PR #157) was not enough, and giving the interim artifact its own name was the change that worked — run 39717765225 for ea1534f completed with both jobs successful. (2) That still does not keep the site current, because the legacy build overwrites the deployed artifact on every push; the workflow's post-deploy guard detects that and redeploys, but a burst of merges keeps winning the race. (3) The only durable fix is a repository setting: Settings → Pages → Source: GitHub Actions (build_type=workflow), which needs an owner with Pages admin — the workflow token receives HTTP 403 from the Pages configuration API. Until that is changed, a reader who sees old matches should reload after the run finishes; the footer timestamp says which snapshot is being served.
